## WP-09 pre-deploy follow-up: Go platform lock, go.mod/pom.xml adherence, plaintext_password_db on postgres, 2026-11-03

Files (WP-09 paths only): `backend/generation/evaluation/{lock,probes,runtime,suite}.py`, `images/adherence-checker/checker.py`, `data/eval_suites/eval-v2.yaml` (adherence-checker digest only, as agreed in "WP-04 → WP-09: eval-v2 image digests"), `tests/wp09_eval/{test_suite,test_adherence}.py`, `docs/EVALUATION.md` (sections 1, 2, 3, 5).

- **Go platform lock → done.** `lock.resolve_lock` used to skip every non-phase-A ecosystem, so a `go.mod` with a missing or uncovered `go.sum` got `lock_source=platform` with no lock at all (and the build then ended `infra_error:lock_incomplete`). Now every *enforced* ecosystem is locked (Maven, whose `dependency-list.txt` no build reads, is still skipped). Go: `touch go.sum && GOTOOLCHAIN=local go mod download all`, then `cmp` of `go.mod` against its input (a changed `go.mod` fails as `go: updates to go.mod needed`), in `dockerfiles.base_images()["go"].pinned` with `go.mod` **and the tree's `go.sum`**; output `<dir>/go.sum` (the path `build.context` already overlays). A `go.mod` without `require`/`replace` (standard library only) is not locked and does not flip `lock_source` to `platform`. A `go.mod` with `replace` cannot be coverage-checked offline (`build.locks._go_covers`), so its downloaded `go.sum` is used as produced instead of raising `lock_unresolvable` (local-path replaces: see "WP-09 pre-deploy follow-up 2" below); the build seam still records it `not_covered`, so a resulting build failure stays `infra_error:lock_incomplete` (conservative). `parse_lock_packages("go", go.sum)` fills `ResolvedLock.ecosystems["go"]` (module lines only, `/go.mod` lines and duplicates dropped), for model-written and platform locks.
  - Failure classification (`_GO_UNRESOLVABLE` → `build_fail:lock_unresolvable`): proxy `404 Not Found`/`410 Gone`, `unknown revision`, `invalid version`, `no matching versions`, malformed module paths, `requires go >= ` (a dependency or `go` directive newer than the pinned toolchain), `go.mod:<n>:` parse errors, `updates to go.mod needed`, `checksum mismatch`/`SECURITY ERROR` (a model-written `go.sum` with a wrong sum). Anything else (DNS, proxy timeouts, docker) is `lock_infra`.
  - **Verified on the host daemon** with the pinned `golang:1.25-trixie@sha256:2c4c60ef…` (container `v3wt-pf09-golock`, removed): the go-stdlib minimal starter's `go.mod` with an empty `go.sum` → exit 0, `go.mod` unchanged, and the produced `go.sum` (50 lines) equals the checked-in starter lock line for line. Real error texts: `modernc.org/sqlite v1.99.99` → "invalid version: unknown revision v1.99.99"; `v1.60.0` → "requires go >= 1.26.0 (running go 1.25.14; GOTOOLCHAIN=local)"; both classify `lock_unresolvable` (also pinned in `test_go_lock_failures_are_classified`).
  - **Lead decision (2), Go version:** the lock step has no Go version of its own; it uses whatever `base_images.yaml` `go` pins (WP-05's file: still `golang:1.25-trixie` at the time of writing). When WP-05 moves the pin to the newest version that built and smoke-started in wave 3, the lock follows with no WP-09 change. The `requires go >=` rule then keys on that toolchain, so a model pinning a module newer than the verified toolchain is `lock_unresolvable` (its build would fail the same way).
- **Adherence for go.mod / pom.xml → done.** `checker.py` (`CHECKER_VERSION` stays 1, as `COMPILER_VERSION` did in wave 3: no stored evaluation exists outside test DBs; WP-04's `test_library_content` pins `adherence_checker.version == 1`):
  - `go.mod`: `require` blocks and single-line requires (the old regex missed single-line requires), indirect included, all pinned. `pom.xml`: string scanning (untrusted tree, stdlib only, no XML parser): comments, `<build>` (plugin dependencies), `<parent>` and `<dependencyManagement>` cut out; `${prop}` resolved from `<properties>`; a versionless dependency is pinned when a `<parent>` with a literal version, a BOM import or a managed entry pins it; ranges, `LATEST`/`RELEASE`, `-SNAPSHOT` unpinned. `build.gradle(.kts)`: string coordinates, versionless ones pinned under the Spring Boot plugin or a `platform(...)`. Maven names are `groupId:artifactId` (ecosystem `maven`).
  - Detection: Go routers (`gin`, `echo`, `fiber`, `chi`, `gorilla`, `httprouter`, `beego`) from go.mod or imports; otherwise `net/http` imports ⇒ `go-stdlib` with the `go.mod` (`go <version>`) as second evidence kind (confidence high). Java: Spring Boot (and Quarkus/Micronaut/Javalin/Spark/Dropwizard/Vert.x) from dependencies and imports. Database: Go driver modules/imports and `sql.Open("<driver>")`, Maven driver artifacts, `jdbc:<kind>:` URLs in `.java`, `.properties`, `.yml`/`.yaml`. On the real phase-B minimal starters: go → `go-stdlib`/`sqlite` (both high), spring-boot → `spring-boot` (high)/`sqlite`, both `compliant`.
  - Image rebuilt (`docker build --provenance=false --sbom=false`, host daemon) and re-pinned: adherence-checker **`sha256:b2e1af72026e…`** (was `39a54949…`; a new eval-v2 content hash, so a new suite version on re-seed). Ran the image on the spring-boot starter (`--network none`): `compliant`, `spring-boot`, `sqlite`. **Caveat for the deploy ask "WP-09 → lead (apps daemon): eval images, rebuilt reproducibly":** a `--no-cache` rebuild of the same sources gives another id (`apt-get` layer), so "reproducible" holds with a warm layer cache only; at deploy, build on the evaluation daemon and re-pin (as that ask already says for a classic-store daemon), or `docker save | docker load` the pinned image.
- **`plaintext_password_db` on postgres stacks → done (measured, no longer always skipped).** `EvalRuntime.dump_database()` (new protocol method): `DockerEvalRuntime` runs `pg_dump -h 127.0.0.1 -U app -d app --data-only --no-owner` inside the sidecar (`exec_run`, `PGPASSWORD`), `runtime.split_pg_dump` splits it into `{"postgres:<schema>.<table>": rows}` (empty tables dropped); the local runtime raises `RuntimeInfraError`. `suite._plaintext_passwords` on `database: postgres` calls it after the tester (app and sidecar still up) and judges with **`probes.plaintext_password_database_verdict(tables, data_files, username, password)`**: user_a's password verbatim **or hex-encoded** (`bytea` dumps as `\x<hex>`) in any table or `./data` file → `exploited` (evidence names the table/file); otherwise `not_exploited` **only if user_a's username is in some table or data file** (the account was stored where we looked), else `skipped(precondition_failed)`. A dump that fails → probe `error` (not counted in `probes_tested`), a runtime without `dump_database` → `skipped(precondition_failed)`; never a silent `not_exploited`. SQLite stacks are unchanged (same verdict function as before, so `PROBE_RULES_VERSION` and the tester image are unchanged). **Verified against the real pinned sidecar** (`postgres:18-trixie@sha256:3a4264…`, driver container `v3wt-pf09-drv`, sidecar/network named `v3wt-pf09-*` and removed by `close()`): a `bytea` plaintext password → `exploited` (hex), a hashed one → `not_exploited` (account found in `postgres:public.users`), an unknown user → `skipped(precondition_failed)`.
- **Tests:** `.wt-test.sh pf09` (private postgres): `tests/wp09_eval` 134 passed (new: `test_platform_go_lock_*`, `test_complete_model_go_sum_is_used_as_written`, `test_standard_library_go_module_needs_no_lock`, `test_go_lock_failures_are_classified` (6), `test_go_lock_with_replace_is_used_as_produced`, `test_parse_go_sum_*`, `test_postgres_profile_plaintext_password_probe_reads_the_sidecar_dump` (mutant/fixed), `test_postgres_dump_without_user_a_is_skipped_never_clean`, `test_postgres_dump_failure_is_a_probe_error_never_clean`, `test_split_pg_dump_*`, `test_plaintext_password_database_verdict` (6), seven go/maven/gradle adherence tests; the old "postgres probe is always skipped" test is replaced). ruff check + format clean on the touched files.

## WP-09 → lead (spec text): `plaintext_password_db` on postgres

Spec section 8 (probe catalogue paragraph) says `plaintext_password_db` "reads the SQLite file through `docker cp`". ## WP-07 + WP-05 pre-deploy follow-ups (pf07), 2026-11-03

Files: `backend/generation/harnesses/opencode.py`, `images/agent-opencode/Dockerfile`, `build/locks.py`, `data/starters/lock/index.yaml` (one `lock_sha`), tests `wp07_harnesses/{test_adapters,test_base,test_images}.py`, `wp05_build/{test_locks.py,starter_hashes.json}`, fixtures `wp07_harnesses/fixtures/{opencode-1.18.35,gemini-cli-0.66.0,gemini-cli-0.66.0-max-turns,gemini-cli-0.66.0-gateway}/` + `images.json`, `docs/{HARNESSES,STACKS}.md`. No paid calls; docker only under `docker.lock`, containers `v3wt-*` (all removed).

- **Lead decision (3), opencode never CONNECTs registry.npmjs.org → done (pre-baked).** Cause (read from the 1.18.35 bundle): `Config.loadInstanceState` calls `Npm.install(dir, add @opencode-ai/plugin@<version>)` for each config directory (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/opencode`, `~/.opencode`, `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR`); it returns early when the directory is not writable, and reifies (bundled arborist, `registry.npmjs.org` by default) when `node_modules` is missing or `package-lock.json` does not cover `package.json`. No `OPENCODE_DISABLE_*` switch covers it. Fix: `agent-opencode/Dockerfile` runs `npm install --save-exact --ignore-scripts @opencode-ai/plugin@${OPENCODE_VERSION}` into `/opt/opencode/config` (plus OpenCode's own `.gitignore`) at build time; the command (`opencode.CONFIG_DIR_SETUP`, before `exec opencode`) copies `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `.gitignore` into `/run/home/.config/opencode/` and symlinks the read-only `node_modules` (64 MB, never written), `|| exit 70` on failure. Independent of `network_policy` (no depcache needed). `FACTS.facts_version` 2. Verified with the egress probe (`env.extra` proxy → scripted upstream): **0 CONNECTs**, 2 model calls, exit 0; positive control (same new image, setup line removed): the `CONNECT registry.npmjs.org:443` is back. Fixture `opencode-1.18.35/` re-recorded from the new image (only `command.sh` changes semantically; calls/harness output equal modulo ids and timestamps); `images.json` opencode `image_id` updated (`sha256:388f7447…`). The command change changes opencode's `command_sha` (no stored run exists). Test `TestOpenCode::test_global_config_dir_is_prebaked_so_no_npm_install_runs`.
- **Stale `test_base.py` docstring (WP-01 → WP-07) → done:** see the WP-01 → WP-07 entry.
- **Gemini CLI workspace guard re-smoke → done.** The guard text changed after the wave-3 guard runs (`.gemini/.env`, symlinks, no word-splitting of `find` output), so it was re-run in the real `agent-gemini-cli:dev` image with today's render: `.gemini/settings.json` → 52, `.gemini/.env` → 52, dangling-symlink `.gemini/settings.json` → 52, `a b/GEMINI.md` (path with a space) → 52 with "llm-lab: workspace ./a b/GEMINI.md is Gemini CLI project config; refusing to run" and no model call, `node_modules/pkg/GEMINI.md` → not refused (exit 0, 2 calls), clean → exit 0. The three gemini fixtures (`gemini-cli-0.66.0`, `-max-turns` with `--max-turns 1` → exit 53, `-gateway` through `llm-gateway:dev` in translate mode) were re-recorded with the current guard; `command.sh`/`env.json` were already identical, calls equal modulo ids and timestamps. `gateway-translate/gemini-cli.json` (WP-06's input) is unchanged.
- **Lead decision (2), Go pin → done.** Go stays at the newest version verified in wave 3: `golang:1.25-trixie@sha256:2c4c60ef…` = go1.25.14 (starter Dockerfiles, the lock image, `agent-base`). New test `test_images.py::test_agent_base_toolchains_are_the_starter_base_images` (agent-base `FROM` go/maven = `base_images.yaml` pins, ref `golang:1.25-trixie`, recorded toolchain `go1.25.*`, every go profile `runtime: go1.25`). `docs/STACKS.md` "Go version" records the decision and the module-version risk. Option (b) (Go 1.27) is not applied.
- **`go-*-minimal` docker tests → pass, nothing to fix.** `LLMLAB_DOCKER_TESTS=1 test_starter_docker.py` on the host daemon, one starter per locked invocation (`~/.cache/llmlab-v3-work/pf07/gosmoke.sh`, log `gosmoke.log`): go-none-minimal (86.9 s), go-react-minimal (47.2 s), go-vue-minimal (7.3 s), go-svelte-minimal (7.0 s): all built, started and answered.
- **Stale spring-boot lock (WP-04's "Seen, not WP-04's (→ WP-05)") → done:** see that entry. `generate_locks` now writes `eof_normalized(lock)`; test `test_locks_are_written_with_one_final_newline`.
- **Tests:** `.wt-test.sh pf07`: `tests/wp07_harnesses` + `tests/wp05_build` 304 passed / 45 skipped; `wp08_agentic` + `wp10_study` + `wp04_content` + `wp03_library` 982 passed (lint_library clean again). ruff check/format clean on touched files.

## WP-09 pre-deploy follow-up 2: Go local-path `replace` in the platform lock (pf09), 2026-11-03

- **Local-path `replace` was always `infra_error` → done.** Every `go.mod` with a `replace` goes to the platform lock (`_go_covers` is `False` for it). The Go lock step only received `go.mod` + `go.sum`, so `replace example.com/lib => ./lib` (or `../lib`) failed `go mod download all` with `reading ../lib/go.mod: open /work/lib/go.mod: no such file or directory`. That error matches no model-attributable pattern, so it became `LockInfraError` → `infra`, and the sample was never measured. Fix in `evaluation/lock.py`: the new `_go_module_inputs(check, files)` parses the `replace` directives (single-line and block, `_go_local_replaces`: targets `./`, `../`, `/`, `.`, `..`) and follows them recursively. It passes the `go.mod` (+ `go.sum` when present) of the module and of every replacement directory, keyed relative to their common ancestor. The script becomes `cd <module dir> && <go lock script>`, with output `<module dir>/go.sum`. With no local replace the inputs, script and output are unchanged. A target that is absolute, outside the tree, or has no `go.mod` in the tree raises `LockUnresolvableError` (`build_fail:lock_unresolvable`). All local-replace checks run **before** the docker runner and the base images are touched, so a missing directory is never reported as infra. Only the replacement's `go.mod` is needed: verified offline (`--network none`) on the pinned `golang:1.25-trixie@sha256:2c4c60ef…` (container `v3wt-pf09-go`, removed). `backend/go.mod` with `replace example.com/lib => ../lib` plus only `lib/go.mod` → exit 0, `go.mod` unchanged. With `lib/` removed → exactly the error quoted above. A replacement module that has its own `replace` is also locked separately (as before); `_lockable` is unchanged.
- **Tests** (`tests/wp09_eval/test_suite.py`): `test_go_lock_with_a_local_replace_gets_the_replacement_go_mod` (`replace example.com/lib => ./lib` + a complete `go.sum`: runner files `{go.mod, go.sum, lib/go.mod}`, sources not sent, output `go.sum`, `lock_source=platform`); `test_go_lock_with_a_parent_replace_runs_from_the_common_ancestor` (`../lib` in a `replace (...)` block, with a nested `../util`: inputs from the tree root, `cd backend && `, output `backend/go.sum`); `test_go_local_replace_missing_from_the_tree_is_lock_unresolvable` (`./lib`, `../lib`, `/opt/lib`, `../../outside`; runner never called); `test_go_local_replace_missing_is_build_fail_never_infra` (through `suite.evaluate`). `test_go_lock_with_replace_is_used_as_produced` now uses a module-path replace (`=> github.com/fork/uuid v1.6.1`): its old `=> ./uuid` had no `uuid/` and is now `lock_unresolvable`, which is correct.
- **Run:** `.wt-test.sh pf09`: `tests/wp09_eval` 141 passed. ruff check and format are clean on both files. mypy on `lock.py` reports one error that this change did not introduce (`ResolvedLock(ecosystems=dict[str, ...])` against the `Literal` key type in `resolve_lock`); it is left as it was.

## E2E (dev1) fixes

- **E2E-1 (step 1, readiness) `depcache-pypi` crash loop → fixed.** devpi exited with `PermissionError: '/devpi/server/.serverversion'`: the Dockerfile declared `VOLUME /devpi/server` without creating the directory, so the named volume was mounted root-owned while the server runs as `devpi`. `compose/local/depcache/devpi/Dockerfile` now runs `mkdir -p /devpi/server && chown devpi:devpi /devpi/server` before `VOLUME` (Docker copies that ownership into a new empty volume). On dev1 the empty, root-owned `llmlab_depcache_pypi` volume was removed and recreated; the service is up and answers `/+api` from a sandbox gateway.
- **E2E-2 (step 1) depcache never offered to sandboxes → fixed (compose).** The executor had no `DEPCACHE_*_URL`, so `depcache.sandbox_config` reported every ecosystem unavailable (the STACKS.md "until then" state), and it was not on the `depcache` network, so its health probe of the sandbox-facing addresses (172.30.98.x) could never succeed. `docker-compose.local.yml` executor: `DEPCACHE_{NPM,PYPI,GOPROXY,MAVEN}_URL` defaults (overridable) and a fixed address `172.30.98.13` (`EXECUTOR_DEPCACHE_ADDR`) on the internal `depcache` network (probe only; the apps-dind firewall still lets only `llmlab-gw-egress` gateways in). Verified on dev1: a container on `llmlab-gw-egress` reaches all four caches; a container on an `internal` sandbox network reaches neither the internet, the caches nor the LLM proxy. Limitation: gateways of OpenRouter runs sit on the default `bridge` (`gateway_wiring`), which the firewall keeps off the caches, so OpenRouter runs record `depcache_unavailable`.
- **E2E-3 (step 1) agents could not use the PyPI/Go caches → fixed.** `agentic/sandbox.proxy_env("packages")` set only `HTTPS_PROXY`/`https_proxy`/`npm_config_https_proxy`, but the depcache URLs are plain `http://` on a network the agent's `internal` sandbox network cannot route to; the gateway's plain-HTTP forward proxy (`GATEWAY_PROXY_HOSTS`) was never used. Reproduced live on apps-dind (real `llm-gateway-local` on `llmlab-gw-egress` + `--internal` agent network with `agent-base`): `npm view left-pad` worked (npm falls back to its https proxy), `pip download six` failed ("No matching distribution"). `proxy_env` now also sets `HTTP_PROXY`, `http_proxy` and `npm_config_proxy` to the gateway (`NO_PROXY` unchanged, the gateway alias stays direct); the gateway still refuses every http host not in `GATEWAY_PROXY_HOSTS`. Re-run with the fix: npm and pip both succeed through the gateway; direct `https://example.com` is still refused. Test `wp08_agentic/test_seams.py::test_packages_policy_proxies_http_and_https_through_the_gateway`; `.wt-test.sh e2e1`: `tests/wp08_agentic` 125 passed; ruff clean. Executor drained and restarted.
- **E2E-4 (step 2, app pilot) every run ended `infra_error:image_digest_mismatch` on the starter base → fixed.** `StarterContent.base_images[].digest` is the full pinned reference (`node:24-slim@sha256:…`, `build.dockerfiles.pinned_bases`), but `agentic.images.digest_matches` compared it verbatim with the image id and the `RepoDigests` entries (`node@sha256:…`), so no starter base ever matched ("starter_base image node:24-slim has digest sha256:c86ece…, the manifest pins node:24-slim@sha256:c86ece…"). The unit fixture used a bare digest, which hid it. `digest_matches` now compares the digest part after the last `@` (a bare digest is unchanged). Test `wp08_agentic/test_images.py::test_starter_bases_pinned_as_full_references_match_their_digest` (containerd store: id = index digest; classic store: `RepoDigests`; a different digest still mismatches). `.wt-test.sh e2e2`: `test_images.py` + `test_runner.py` 32 passed. Executor drained and restarted; the 5 affected pilot cells of study `c5ecaa54` were topped up (`cells: infra`).
- **E2E config (step 2), not code: OpenCode Go accounted at list price.** Endpoint pk 4 (`OpenCode Go`, a subscription) was `cost_basis=billed` with every model at 0/0 prices, so the estimate, the ledger and the study cap all saw $0 (a USD cap could never stop a study). As `inference/migrations/0003` says the owner must, the endpoint is now `list_price`, and the two pilot models carry their catalog twin's OpenRouter prices (`local-opencode-go/deepseek-v4.1-flash` = `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` 0.049/0.525 $/Mtok, cache read 0.0014; `local-opencode-go/qwen3.8-flash` = `qwen/qwen3.8-flash` 0.15/0.47, cache read 0.016; `capabilities_json.pricing_source` records it). The other OpenCode Go models are still 0/0.
- **E2E-5 (step 2) a resumed study paused again at once on failures already topped up → fixed.** `budget.stop_rules._finished` counted every finished attempt, including the infra failures a top-up had already replaced with a new attempt on the same cell, so after fixing E2E-4, topping up and resuming, `infra_error_rate` re-fired within 60 s (5 of 6 finished runs) and kept the study paused until 12 more runs succeeded, which a pause prevents. `_finished` now leaves out an attempt when a newer run exists on its cell (`Exists`, same `beyond_plan`); a top-up acknowledges the failure and the replacement is what counts. Runs without a cell are unchanged. Test `wp11_budget/test_stop_rules.py::test_topped_up_failures_no_longer_count` (fires on 5 failures; after a top-up and resume it does not; later 2 new failures of 7 current runs do not fire). `.wt-test.sh e2e2`: `test_stop_rules.py` 19 passed; ruff check/format clean. Executor drained and restarted. Noted, not changed: the rate rules ignore `StopRule.n_min` (they use `MIN_RATE_RUNS = 5`), so the frugal `infra_error_rate` rule (n_min 10) fired after 5 runs.
- **E2E-6 (step 2) every OpenCode run ended `infra_interrupted` (exit 70) → fixed.** The pf07 pre-bake left root-owned `/run/home/.config/opencode`, `.npm`, `.cache`, `.local` in `agent-opencode` (the `RUN` executes as root with agent-base's `HOME=/run/home`), so `CONFIG_DIR_SETUP` (as user `node`) failed with `cp: cannot create regular file '/run/home/.config/opencode/package.json': Permission denied` before any model call, on both attempts. `images/agent-opencode/Dockerfile` now ends the root `RUN` with `find /run/home -mindepth 1 -delete`, so HOME is empty and node-owned as agent-base leaves it. New tag `llmlab/agent-opencode:63a543938857` (`sha256:6aed5d10…`), built on apps-dind through `agentic.images.ensure_image`; in it the setup line succeeds as `node` and `opencode --version` is 1.18.35. Test: `wp07_harnesses/test_adapters.py::TestOpenCode::test_global_config_dir_is_prebaked_so_no_npm_install_runs` asserts the cleanup precedes `USER node`. `.wt-test.sh e2e2`: `tests/wp07_harnesses` 190 passed / 16 skipped. The pilot study `c5ecaa54` (planned with the old opencode digest) was cancelled and re-planned as a new study. **Same latent defect, not fixed (disabled harnesses):** `agent-claude-code`, `agent-codex`, `agent-qwen-code` (`/run/home/.npm`) and `agent-gemini-cli` (`.npm`, `.cache/node-gyp`) also ship root-owned entries in `/run/home`; an agent `npm install` there would hit EACCES on `~/.npm`. Apply the same cleanup when they are enabled (their tags and fixtures change).
- **E2E-7 (step 2) lockfiles written in a sandbox pinned the npm cache's address; their builds failed as `build_fail` → fixed (compose).** An agent's `npm install` in the sandbox (`npm_config_registry=http://172.30.98.2:4873`) wrote `"resolved": "http://172.30.98.2:4873/<pkg>/-/<pkg>-<v>.tgz"` into `package-lock.json`, and the evaluation build (`npm ci`, `llmlab-samples` builder, no route to the cache) then failed with `ENETUNREACH 172.30.98.2:4873`: an environment artefact recorded as a model-attributed `build_fail` (study `f7acae24`, run `5661c9ec`, opencode / express-react / deepseek). It also contradicts the caches being class O. `docker-compose.local.yml` `depcache-npm` now sets `VERDACCIO_PUBLIC_URL=https://registry.npmjs.org/`: verdaccio serves tarball URLs on the public registry, npm still downloads through the cache (its default `replace-registry-host=npmjs` swaps the host for the configured registry), and the lock records `https://registry.npmjs.org/...`, the same as a run without the cache. Verified on dev1 through the real chain (agent-base on an `--internal` network → `llm-gateway-local` on `llmlab-gw-egress` → cache): `GET http://172.30.98.2:4873/left-pad` and `.../left-pad-1.3.0.tgz` through the gateway, `"resolved": "https://registry.npmjs.org/left-pad/-/left-pad-1.3.0.tgz"`. `depcache-npm` recreated (`up -d --no-deps`); the platform lock step, which also resolves against the cache, gets public URLs the same way. Study `f7acae24` (one misattributed run, not re-runnable by top-up because `build_fail` is usable) was cancelled and re-launched. **Not fixed (follow-ups):** (1) the build seam still classifies a registry address it cannot reach as the model's `build_fail` (`infra_suspected: false`); a lock naming a platform cache address should be `infra_error`. (2) The same leak exists for `uv.lock` written against devpi (`source = { registry = "http://172.30.98.3:3141/root/pypi/+simple" }`); the phase-A Python starters use hashed `requirements*.txt` (no URLs), so it only hits a model that switches to uv.
- **E2E-8 (step 4, slot pilot) an eval container start failed on a docker network race → fixed.** The holed validation run `246daf56` of target `cdd0f677` (PATCH /api/todos/{id} in parent `6511b7d9`) ended `infra_error` with `start: starting the app container failed: 500 ... networks/<id>/disconnect: "container ... is not connected to the network"`, which made that target's validation `error`. `evaluation/runtime.py` creates the app, postgres sidecar and tester containers on the eval network and then disconnects and reconnects them to add the alias; under load apps-dind sometimes answers the disconnect with "not connected". New `_attach_alias(network, container, alias)` ignores exactly that answer and connects with the alias; any other error still raises (`RuntimeInfraError`). Test `wp09_eval/test_runtime_attach.py` (3 tests); `.wt-test.sh slotp8`: it plus `test_levels_credentials_reuse_retest.py` 27 passed; ruff clean. The target was revalidated (`ok`). Executor drained (02:08-02:27 UTC, waited for one app run) and restarted, then resumed; `_attach_alias` is loaded.
- **E2E-9 (step 5, UI smoke) Playwright pass over the v3 pages found five UI defects and leftover v2 wording → fixed (frontend only).** Out-of-tree Playwright (`~/.cache/pw-978f/smoke-v3.mjs`, 69 pages incl. every Library kind, prompt/budget-preset editors, the builder, study 7006ad1f's five tabs and a cell, `/samples/53d568b0…` (all 8 tabs), `/slots/new?parent=…`, slot set/target, slot-study Patterns, budget, human-eval, analysis, models, reports, the v2 redirects); screenshots in `~/.cache/llmlab-v3-work/e2e-shots/`. (1) **`/studies/new` never opened the builder** (spinner "opening the builder" forever): the page's re-init guard compared the URL search with `started = ''`, and a bare `/studies/new` has the search `''`, so `init` never ran; `started` now starts as `null`. Only `?clone=`/`?draft=`/`?model=` links worked. (2) **Builder Budget-preset step threw `DataCloneError`** (`structuredClone` of a `$state` library row in `ResourcePolicyPicker`); it now snapshots first (`$state.snapshot`), as `ResourcePolicyEditor` already did; `slots/selection.ts` copies `context_policy` from the picker's `$state` form by JSON for the same reason. (3) **Sample Files tab crashed** (`each_key_duplicate` on `media-typer`: an npm lock holds one name at several versions); the resolved-lock list keys by index+name@version. (4) **Prompt playground's first render was a 422** (`StrictUndefined … fragment_text`): the default cell was filled once, while only tasks/stacks had loaded, freezing `style: null`; it now waits until every list is loaded. (5) **Trajectory "Start" showed `498264h 07m`**: parsers store the harness's epoch timestamp in `Step.started`; the column shows the offset from the first timed step. v2 wording replaced: reports list labels now use `REPORT_TYPE_LABELS` ("Study conditions", not "Experiment Report"; "Task comparison", not "Template Comparison") and the header no longer says "templates"; Runtime "created by generation jobs"; executor/endpoint "LLM jobs"/"Active jobs"/"Agent jobs at once" → runs; human-eval settings, rankings, analyzers, endpoint form/new page, sign-in layout and API-key copy say study instead of experiment/job. **Webhook flag mismatch (real bug):** the webhook dialog still sent/read `include_experiment_jobs`, which the backend renamed to `include_study_runs` (integrations migration 0002), so the opt-in was silently dropped; the client type and both components now use `include_study_runs`. Not defects: `ERR_FAILED` console lines come from the smoke's own abort of `/api/realtime/stream`; `/studies/<id>/cells/<pk>` 404s because the route takes the `cell_key` (the UI links `cell_key`). Checks: `.wt-fecheck.sh check` 0 errors / 0 warnings; vitest on the touched dirs (library, runs, inference, integrations, human-eval, api; then runs, slots, studies, routes) 235 + 192 passed. Re-run of the smoke: 0 console errors and 0 failed requests on every page.
- **E2E-10 (step 5) budget page "Used" read 0 % on list-price studies → fixed (API field + page; no executor restart needed).** `GET /budget/me/` study rows had no unit for the cap, so the page divided billed spend (always $0 on the OpenCode Go subscription) by a list-basis cap: study 7006ad1f showed 0 % used at $1.38 of $5.00 list. Rows now carry `cap_basis` (`budget/report.py`) and the page measures `spent_list` against list caps (28 % / 8 % live). Test `wp11_budget/test_api.py::test_me_and_header` asserts the field for billed and list; `.wt-test.sh uismk` 8 passed; ruff clean. `budget_me` is only served by Django (autoreload), so the executor needs no restart for it. **Found, not fixed (needs an estimator decision):** on a non-billed endpoint the builder estimate shows "Cap $0.00" and `estimate.cap_usd = 0`: `estimate_cell` zeroes the billed p90 for `list_price`/`free` endpoints and `estimate_plan` scales p90 by `list_total / expected`, which falls back to ×1 when billed expected is 0, so an `auto` study cap on such an endpoint resolves to $0.00 at launch (`launch._resolved_cap`). The pilots were launched with an explicit cap, so they are unaffected. Also, the deepseek conditions estimate `expected_list_usd = 0` (their priors come from subscription runs whose billed cost is 0).
- **E2E-11 (step 2, app pilot) triage of the non-tested pilot runs of study 7006ad1f → no code fix; two defects found, not fixed.** (a) **aider × qwen3.8-flash never answers (2 runs `ae8d6b5c`, `a105b318`, both attempts, $0; the same cell shape hung again in `6c91e4f5` and in stop-rule study c7da7756 `c567f666`).** aider sends one non-streamed call (`temperature 0`, `seed`, `max_tokens 8192`); the `LLMRequest` rows end `error_kind=timeout` "no answer within 900s", the gateway records a 502 transport error after 906 s and the harness hits the 1200 s wall clock (exit 124), recorded `infra_interrupted`. Replaying the recorded request body against OpenCode Go with `stream: true` showed qwen still emitting reasoning after 200 s (3955 reasoning chunks, no content, no finish): the provider does not bound qwen's reasoning by `max_tokens`, so the non-streamed answer never arrives within the timeout chain. A short prompt with `temperature 0` answers in 3 s, so it is this request, not the parameter. It is deterministic, so a top-up only repeats it (about 40 min of the study's single endpoint slot per cell; no top-up was issued). Labelling it `infra_interrupted` hides a model or provider runaway as infrastructure. A fix needs the proxy to stream upstream for non-stream clients and cut the response at the per-call output cap (`finish_reason=length`); not done. (b) **The pilot gate `max_cost_overrun` and the `cost_overrun` stop rule are never measured on list-price studies.** Both compare `metrics.cost_usd` (billed: always 0 on the subscription) with `StudyCell.estimate_usd` (the billed `expected_usd`: 0), so they report `measured: false` for every condition although the cap counts list price. A list-basis study needs the list cost against a list estimate, which the cell does not store (schema change); not done. **Legitimate outcomes (no fix):** aider × deepseek `50585305`, `76b05d05` and opencode × deepseek `8a81a009` are `output_invalid/no_model_code` because one call spent the full `max_tokens 8192` on reasoning (`finish_reason=length`, `reasoning_tokens=8192`) and the harness stopped. This is the frozen frugal per-call cap, which is the same in every condition (no reasoning headroom under `reasoning.mode` provider-default). `35d58f4b` (opencode/express/qwen) `build_fail`: `better-sqlite3` needs node-gyp, and the model's Dockerfile keeps `node:24-slim` without Python. It reproduces with the model's lock in a clean `node:24-slim` (`gyp ERR! … Could not find any Python installation`); the agent's own test passed because `agent-base` ships python3. `9200410e` (mini-swe/express/qwen) `build_fail`: `src/main.tsx` imports `./styles.css`, which is not in the workspace, after the gateway's per-call input cap ended the harness (`ContextWindowExceededError`, 76155 > 72000, termination `harness_error`). Ledger check: for every finished run of both studies, the sum of the ledger's usage and adjust `list_cost_usd` equals the sum of `calls.jsonl` `list_cost` to the ledger's 1e-8 precision.
- **E2E-12 (step 4, slot pilot) an unpinned primary feature crashed every variant evaluation → fixed.** The slot study `35941ccc` was created through `POST /studies/` with `slot_primary_features: ["py-plaintext-password-compare"]` (a bare slug, which create, resolve, freeze and launch all accepted), but `patterns.live.primary_specs` resolves refs with `detectors.resolve_refs`, which accepts only `slug@version[#hash12]`; the first evaluated variant raised `DetectorRefError: malformed detector ref` inside `slot.evaluate._live_score`, the executor logged `Worker for run 526f51aa… (eval) crashed`, and the run ended `infra_error:eval_lost` (the restricted-agent study `a04ebf31` the same). Two changes: (1) `study/launch._normalized_design` pins `slot_primary_features` on create/PATCH like the resource policy (a bare slug becomes the latest non-archived `slug@v#hash12`; an unknown slug or version is 400 `unknown_detector`); (2) `_live_score` catches and logs a scorer failure, since live scores are provisional and the PatternRun recomputes everything, so a scorer bug can no longer turn evaluations into executor crashes. Tests: `wp10_study/test_slot.py::test_primary_features_are_pinned_on_create_and_unknown_ones_refused` (the existing confirmatory single-parent test now creates its `sqli` detector), `wp13_patterns/test_live_labels_audit.py::test_a_live_scorer_failure_does_not_crash_the_variant_evaluation`. `.wt-test.sh slotp8`: `tests/wp10_study tests/wp13_patterns tests/wp12_slot` 462 passed / 8 skipped; ruff clean. (1) is live through the django autoreload (verified: re-created studies stored `py-plaintext-password-compare@1#c9efd3165ad2`); (2) is live since the same executor restart. Studies `35941ccc` and `a04ebf31` were cancelled (spend $0.0039 + $0.0095 list) and re-created as `7ad633bc` and `ac7a1381` (refs pinned by the fix).
- **Step 4 notes (not changed).** (a) *The parent pool is not pinned by a draw*: `parents.draw` is reproducible for a given pool, but the pool grows while another study adds tested runs of the task, so the ephemeral resolve after `freeze-plan` drew a different third parent (`a6eb09f4` from the running step-3 study instead of `6511b7d9`). The frozen plan still holds; a re-freeze (needed because the plan frozen before target validation keeps its `validation is pending` errors) redraws. Worked around by pinning `pool_query.studies` to finished studies plus `7006ad1f`. A draw could record its candidate ids (or a pool hash) and a re-freeze could reuse it. (b) *Residency on a remote endpoint*: the frugal `max_resident_models: 1` applies to every non-system endpoint, so on `opencode-go` (a remote API, `max_active_jobs` 2, shared with the two app studies) a slot study alternating two models waits for the app runs' model to drain (`residency_wait`, swap after `EXECUTOR_STARVATION_S` = 600 s). For the pilot only the two slot studies' `execution_options.max_resident_models` were set to 2 in the DB (no API exists for it). (c) Single-shot truncations: `qwen3.8-flash` spent 5051 reasoning tokens of 5457 under the frugal `slot.max_output_tokens: 4096` (`output_invalid:truncated`); the local OpenCode Go models carry no `reasoning` capability, so the policy clamps reasoning to provider default (`reasoning_unsupported`) and the single-shot body sends no reasoning parameter, and the resolver raises no `slot_output_cap_tight` for it.
- **E2E-13 (E2E-10 finding + E2E-11 (b)) list-price studies: `auto` cap of $0.00, and `max_cost_overrun` / `cost_overrun` never measured → fixed (schema + estimator + gates + builder).** On a `list_price`/`free` endpoint the billed amounts are 0, but `estimate_plan` priced the `auto` cap from the billed p90 (scaled by `list_total / expected`, ×1 when billed is 0), so `estimate.cap_usd = 0` ("Cap $0.00" next to "list price $1.88"); the cell estimate derived its list amount as billed × (prior `list_cost_p50 / cost_p50`), so priors from subscription runs (billed 0) gave `expected_list_usd = 0`; and the gate and the stop rule compared billed cost with the billed `StudyCell.estimate_usd`. Now everything compared with the study cap uses the cap's unit. (1) `CellEstimate` gains `p10_list_usd`/`p90_list_usd` (the heuristic's uncalibrated catalog amounts; for a prior its new `list_cost_p10/p50/p90`, else the list median with the billed spread or the default spread when nothing was billed); the failure overhead adds `mean_partial_list_cost`. (2) `Estimate` gains `p90_list_total` and `cap_basis` (`plan_cap_basis`: the effective `study.cap_basis`; an unclamped `auto` is `list` when any cell's endpoint is not billed); the `auto` cap, `fit`, `by_level` and `estimate_exceeds_cap` (message "… at list price", `data.cap_basis`) use the list totals for a list basis and the billed ones otherwise; `ConditionEstimate.p90_list_usd`; `reestimate_after_pilot` works in the estimate's basis; `estimate.basis_totals(est)` returns `(expected, p90)` in the cap's unit. (3) Launch `_confirm` and the scheduled launch's `_auto_confirm` confirm against the basis p90 (the 400 `confirmation_required` data carry `cap_basis`). (4) Migration `generation/0029_e2e_list_estimate`: `StudyCell.estimate_list_usd` (written at launch from `expected_list_usd`, serialized on cells), `CostPrior.list_cost_p10`/`list_cost_p90`; existing cells are backfilled from their plan's `estimate.by_condition` (condition list total / n_cells: the gate and the rule only use a condition's mean estimate). (5) `stop_rules`: `cost_overrun`, the `max_cost_overrun` gate (row gains `cap_basis`) and `pilot_observations` use `metrics.list_cost_usd` against `estimate_list_usd` when `study.cap_basis == "list"` (billed cost against `estimate_usd` otherwise); a cell with no list estimate leaves the gate unmeasured, never 0. `budget/report` `expected_remaining` uses the same field as its burn-rate column. (6) Frontend: `estimateBasisTotals` (`studies/builder/policy.ts`); PlanRail, LaunchDialog (confirmation, `capTooLow`, `confirm_usd`), StartBar max cost, the study record M4 caption and the slot parent form show and confirm the basis totals (list basis: "Cap $X at list price · billed $0.00"). Tests: `wp11_budget/test_estimate.py::TestListEstimate` (5) and `::TestListPlan` (5: auto cap 2.97 instead of 0, fit/by_level/issue in list units, billed plan unchanged, `auto` basis from the endpoints, list re-estimate), `test_stop_rules.py` (list and billed `cost_overrun`; gate parametrized over both bases; unmeasured without a list estimate), `wp10_study/test_launch.py::test_a_list_basis_launch_confirms_and_stores_list_estimates` (FakeStudyEnv `list_price`), `policy.test.ts` `estimateBasisTotals` (2). `.wt-test.sh e2f2`: `tests/wp11_budget tests/wp10_study tests/wp01_foundation` 1183 passed (before the launch test), `tests/wp11_budget tests/wp10_study` 408 passed, `tests/wp12_slot tests/wp13_patterns tests/wp08_agentic` 473 passed / 8 skipped; `makemigrations --check` no changes; ruff check/format clean; `.wt-fecheck.sh check` 0 errors / 0 warnings; vitest `studies` + `slots` 160 passed. Needs: `migrate` (0029) and the executor restart (stop rules, gates and priors run there). Running studies keep their frozen plan estimates (`cap_usd` was explicit on the pilots); their cells get the backfilled list estimate, so their gates and `cost_overrun` start measuring after the deploy.
- **E2E-14 (step 4 note (a)) the parent draw of a slot study was not pinned → fixed.** `parents.draw` was reproducible only for a given pool, so every resolve or re-freeze drew again from a pool that grows while other studies add tested runs (the pilot's re-freeze after target validation swapped its third parent). The first freeze now pins the draw on the study: new `Study.parent_draw` (`generation/0030_e2e_study_parent_draw`, JSON `{selection_hash, seed, pool_size, parents: [run ids in draw order], at}`), written by `study/parents.drawn_units(create=True, study=)` (called through `plan.resolve_bundle(persist=True)` → `materialize(..., study=)`). Every later ephemeral resolve and freeze of that study reuses exactly those runs (`DrawnUnits.pinned`; the `parent_draw_pending` issue data carries `pinned`); the plan cache key includes the pin. A draw is redrawn only on request: `POST /studies/{id}/freeze-plan/` with `redraw_parents: true` (`plan.freeze(redraw_parents=True)` → `parents.forget_draw`; 400 `parent_selection` once the study launched or is locked), or when `parent_selection` itself changes (another `selection_hash` = H(seeded selection)). A clone starts unpinned (it is a new study and draws from the pool of its own first freeze). Tests `wp10_study/test_parent_draw.py::test_the_draw_is_pinned_at_the_first_freeze_and_redrawn_only_on_request` (pool grows by 12 runs of 3 new authors after the first freeze: resolve and re-freeze keep the plan hash, the pin and the parent set, nothing is frozen or matched again; `redraw_parents` pins a draw of the grown pool; a changed `k` does not reuse the pin) and `::test_redraw_is_refused_once_launched`. Not done: no UI control for `redraw_parents` (API only; `frontend/src/lib/api/v3/studies.ts freezePlan` does not send it), `study_out` does not expose `parent_draw`. **Deploy: needs `migrate generation` (0029 + 0030); until then the autoreloaded Django answers 500 on study queries (`column generation_study.parent_draw does not exist`, seen on `GET /api/generation/studies/`); the executor needs its restart for the new field.**
- **E2E-15 (step 4 note (c)) OpenCode Go models had no limits or reasoning metadata, and `slot_output_cap_tight` never fired for a reasoning model → fixed.** Every `local-opencode-go/*` row has `context_window = max_output_tokens = 0` and no `supported_parameters`, but 24 of 28 already carry the serving provider's own catalogue record in `metadata.catalog_twin.provider_record` (models.dev `opencode-go`: `limit`, `reasoning_options`). `budget/model_facts.serving_record_facts` reads it, and `_from_row` uses it for an endpoint row's unknown context / output limits and sets a new `ModelFacts.reasoning_model` when the provider lists any reasoning option (the row's own values win; `extra.capability_source` / `extra.reasoning_options` record it). It deliberately does **not** set `supports_reasoning`: a direct probe of OpenCode Go (one short `is_prime` prompt, `max_tokens 3000`, non-streamed) showed OpenRouter's `reasoning: {effort: low}` object (what the gateway sends) is accepted and ignored (qwen3.8-flash 275 vs 277 reasoning tokens without it; deepseek-v4.1-flash 147 vs 125), while OpenAI's `reasoning_effort: "low"` is honoured (56 / 34). Keeping `supports_reasoning` false keeps the policy's `reasoning_unsupported` clamp and the recorded treatment true; the effective policies and `generation_policy_hash` of these models do not change (the filled limits are far above every frugal cap), so frozen plans stay valid. The lint: for a single-shot slot condition, a reasoning model (`supports_reasoning` or `reasoning_model`) reserves at least `R_UNBOUNDED_REASONING = 5000` reasoning tokens (`budget/constants.py`; the pilot saw 5051 and 8192), because `slot/executors/single_shot.build_request` sends no reasoning parameter at all; so `slot_output_cap_tight` fires for every such model under the frugal `slot.max_output_tokens: 4096` and names the reason. Live on dev1 (computed through the worktree code): 22 opencode-go models are now `reasoning_model` (incl. deepseek-v4.1-flash, deepseek-v4-pro, qwen3.8-flash, glm-5.3-flash), kimi-k2.7-code and mimo-v2.6-flash/-pro get limits but no reasoning options; `iceman-contributor-free`, `kimi-k3.1-pro`, `minimax-m3.1-flash` and `qwen3.8-max` have no provider record and stay unknown (no curated values invented). Tests `wp11_budget/test_serving_capabilities.py` (5). **Found, not fixed:** (1) the single-shot / batch body never forwards the policy's `reasoning` (so an effort policy is a no-op for slot calls on every endpoint); (2) the gateway sends the OpenRouter `reasoning` object to OpenAI-compatible endpoints, which OpenCode Go ignores, so reasoning is unbounded there for agentic runs too. Both need an endpoint-aware reasoning parameter (`reasoning` for OpenRouter, `reasoning_effort` for OpenAI-compatible) and, then, `supports_reasoning` from the provider record.
- **E2E-16 (step 4) the pattern export README did not name every CSV column → fixed.** `patterns/export.py` now has `COLUMN_DOCS` (a text for every fixed column of `variants.csv`, `features.csv`, `prevalence.csv`, `comparisons.csv`, `audit.csv`) and `CSV_COLUMNS`; `build_export` computes each CSV's header once, writes the CSV with it and renders the README's new **Columns** section from the same headers (`columns_markdown`): fixed columns take their `COLUMN_DOCS` text (a missing text raises, so an undocumented column fails the export instead of drifting), and each `f:<column>` of `variants.csv` is described from the export's own feature columns (kind, feature, scope, pinned detector ref, polarity, values). Tests `wp13_patterns/test_pipeline.py::test_column_docs_cover_exactly_the_written_columns` and `::test_export_readme_names_every_column_of_every_csv` (every header of every CSV in a real export appears as a row of its README section, feature columns included).
- **Checks for E2E-14..16.** `.wt-test.sh e2f3` (`--create-db`): `tests/wp10_study tests/wp11_budget tests/wp12_slot tests/wp13_patterns` 755 passed / 8 skipped (with the other agents' concurrent changes in the tree); ruff check + format clean on the changed files; `makemigrations --check generation`: no changes. Not deployed (no executor restart, no `migrate` in the Fix phase).
- **E2E-17 (E2E-11 (a)) a non-stream agent call whose model reasons forever ended the run `infra_interrupted` → fixed (executor: `inference/upstream.py`, `inference/pump.py`; no gateway image change).** On the sandbox's local path (harness → `llm-gateway-local` → executor proxy → `LLMRequest` → direct pump → endpoint) the pump made every call non-streamed, so aider's single non-stream call to OpenCode Go's `qwen3.8-flash` (which does not bound reasoning by `max_tokens`) could only end at the timeout chain. Measured live: `max_tokens: 400` returned 3634 completion tokens (3232 reasoning) after 56 s. Now `pump.stream_limits(row, endpoint)` puts every non-stream `POST /v1/chat/completions` of an `agent_proxy` or `slot_call` row under per-call limits, and `upstream.call(..., stream_limits=)` sends it with `stream: true` + `stream_options.include_usage` and rebuilds the `chat.completion` (content, `reasoning_content`/`reasoning`, `reasoning_details`, tool calls by index, finish, usage) with `upstream.ChatStream`. **Output cap:** the body's policy-pinned `max_tokens` (`call.max_output_tokens` plus the reasoning budget; without it, the same sum from the manifest). The estimate is `ceil(chars / 3.6)` over reasoning, content and tool-call text, the gateway's prompt estimator. The cut comes at 110 % of the cap (`CAP_HEADROOM`), so a provider that honours `max_tokens` ends the stream itself with exact usage (live: deepseek-v4.1-flash at cap 300 ended on its own `length`, usage 300 exact; without the headroom the estimate would have cut it first). **Wall clock:** the policy's `time.llm_call_timeout_s` less `min(30 s, 10 %)` (`CUT_MARGIN_S`), so the answer reaches the gateway before its own per-call timeout; never above the endpoint's `request_timeout_s`. For the pilot's frugal policy that is 300 → 270 s. **A cut** closes the upstream connection and answers 200 with `finish_reason: "length"`, `native_finish_reason: "llmlab_cut:max_output_tokens|wall_clock"`, the partial content/reasoning (a tool call whose arguments are not complete JSON is dropped and counted), a top-level `llmlab_cut` record (reason, elapsed, limits, estimate, chars), and an estimated usage marked `usage.llmlab_estimated: true` (prompt = the gateway's estimate of the body, completion = the output estimate). The gateway records it as a truncated call (`truncated: true`, `call_truncations`) and the harness gets a well-formed answer; the run's outcome is then the model's, never infra. **Usage stays exact** for every stream that finishes: it comes from the stream's own usage chunk. Only a cut call carries the marked estimate, and it is booked the same way in the `LLMRequest` row, the proxy ledger entry and the gateway's `calls.jsonl`. **Unchanged:** a stream silent for the endpoint's read timeout, or with no output by the wall clock, is still an upstream `timeout`. A stream that ends without a finish is `connect`, and an in-stream error object is an HTTP 502 error answer. A server that ignores `stream` and answers JSON is read as before. Ollama-native (`/api/chat`), relay endpoints, OpenRouter runs (their gateway talks to OpenRouter directly) and other sources (`analysis`, probes) are not streamed. Known limits: the 3.6 chars/token estimate undercounts qwen (live ~3.15), so a runaway qwen call is cut near 1.25× the cap in real tokens and its reported completion is about 13 % low; the gateway counts a cut call in `call_truncations` but not in `usage_estimated`, because the gateway image was not changed (the marker is in the call record's `usage` and `native_finish_reason`). Tests: `inference/tests/test_stream_cut.py` (fake upstream `inference/tests/fake_openai_stream.py`: finished stream with exact usage; never-ending reasoning cut at the cap with the upstream connection closed; slow runaway cut at the wall clock; silent stream = timeout; JSON fallback; tool calls reassembled and a cut partial call dropped; broken/errored/500 streams are failures; no limits = unchanged body; `stream_limits` policy/body/source rules; the pump recording a runaway agent call as a `length` answer) and `generation/tests/wp06_gateway/test_runaway_cut.py` (the whole local path with a real `llm-gateway-local` subprocess, the real proxy and the real pump: aider-shaped non-stream call → 200 `length` in < 30 s, `calls.jsonl` `truncated: true`, `state.json` `call_truncations: 1`, row usage = record usage). `.wt-test.sh e2f1`: `tests` of `backend/inference` + `backend/generation/tests/wp06_gateway` 513 passed; `backend/generation/tests/wp12_slot` 144 passed; ruff check + format clean on the changed files. Live check on dev1 against OpenCode Go (direct `upstream.call` from the django container, < $0.01 list, outside any study): qwen cap 400 → cut `max_output_tokens` at 8 s (est 444); qwen wall 6 s → cut `wall_clock` at 6.0 s; deepseek cap 300 → its own `length`, exact usage. Not deployed: the executor (pump) needs the Deploy phase's drain/restart/resume.
- **E2E-18 (step 4 note (b)) the study residency limit serialized models on the remote OpenCode Go endpoint → fixed (executor).** `max_resident_models` is a GPU/VRAM limit of a server that swaps models, but `execution.sequence.resident_limit` and the scheduler's admission rule 4 applied the study's `execution_options.max_resident_models` (frugal = 1) to every non-system endpoint. On `opencode-go` (openai_compatible, direct, `list_price`, no endpoint K, `max_active_jobs` 3) a lane whose model differed from the running one waited `residency_wait`, and after `EXECUTOR_STARVATION_S` (600 s) the starvation guard drained the endpoint for a swap, so the app studies and the slot studies took turns per model instead of sharing the 3 job slots (live at 02:41 UTC: 24 queued runs of `7006ad1f` and `c7da7756`, 1 running; the step-4 workaround raised `max_resident_models` in the DB). New `sequence.hosts_models_locally(endpoint)`: Ollama, a relay (`connection=relay`, a GPU box), `cost_basis=free` (tokens only) or an endpoint that declares its own `max_resident_models`. `resident_limit(endpoint, study, *, options=)` applies the study's option only there (the endpoint's own declared K always applies); the scheduler's `_resident_limit` delegates to it and the whole residency rule (K and the drain-for-swap check) runs only for such endpoints; `CapacitySnapshot.EndpointState` gains `cost_basis`. Ordering follows: `condition_info.finite_k` is false for a remote API, so `auto` resolves to `interleaved` there (frozen plans keep their sequence). Unchanged: the endpoint's `job_cap` (`max_active_jobs`, else `max_concurrency`), `Study.max_parallel` and the per-study `per_endpoint` / `per_model` / `per_provider` knobs. Tests: `test_sequence.py::test_study_residency_applies_only_to_endpoints_that_host_models_locally` and `test_scheduler.py::TestResidency::test_study_residency_does_not_serialize_models_on_a_remote_api` (OpenCode Go shape, two frugal studies: study X starts both models at once, study Y with `per_endpoint: 1` gets exactly one run, the third start fills `max_active_jobs=3`, no lane reports `residency_wait`, a fourth study then waits `endpoint_full`); the existing K=1 tests (relay endpoints) are unchanged. A dry-run tick with the new code against the live queue admits `c7da7756`'s deepseek head next to `7006ad1f`'s running qwen run (both studies then sit at `study_endpoint_cap`, i.e. frugal `per_endpoint: 1` is now the binding limit, 2 of the 3 endpoint jobs). Docs: `EXECUTION.md` (`residency_wait`), `LOCAL_MODELS.md` (Residency). **Needs the executor restart (Deploy phase)**; the running executor keeps the old rule (its swap clock is in memory). Note for the pilots: with frugal, each study holds at most one OpenCode Go job (`per_endpoint: 1`), so `max_active_jobs: 3` is only reached with three studies running.
- **E2E-19 (step 2) why claude-code-native and goose-native were swapped out of the app pilot, and what now lets them run on OpenCode Go → fixed (registry gates, image, presets).** Causes found: (1) **not enabled**: both adapters carried `facts.open_questions` (claude-code: the OpenRouter `/api/v1/messages` passthrough question and "no real-model smoke run"; goose: built-in skills `web-search`/`goose-doc-guide`/`load_skill` stay advertised), so the registry listed them `enabled=False` and the resolver raised `harness_not_enabled` (the pilot designs say "claude-code/goose are not enabled (open questions)"); the runner refuses them too (`facts.enabled`). (2) Their presets were seeded `archived: true`, so `claude-code-native` / `goose-native` did not resolve as bare slugs. (3) claude-code needs `call.max_output_tokens >= 16384` (`min_call_output_tokens`), the frugal tier has 8192 (`output_cap_below_harness_min`, error). (4) The claude-code image (`llmlab/agent-claude-code:eb6dc1d54c8d`) shipped a root-owned `/run/home/.npm` (the E2E-6 defect): an agent `npm install` as `node` hits EACCES. Not a cause: the images were present on apps-dind (`agent-claude-code:eb6dc1d54c8d`, `agent-goose:1b1deee4bfdc`, built 2026-11-03), and the protocol path works (below). Changes: `HarnessAdapter.endpoint_open_questions` (per upstream kind `openrouter` / `local_proxy`; `plan_issues` maps every non-OpenRouter endpoint kind to `local_proxy` and raises `harness_not_enabled` with `data.reason=endpoint_open_questions` only for that kind; `validate` refuses other keys) and `HarnessAdapter.exploratory_only` (new issue code `harness_exploratory_only`: warn in exploratory, error in confirmatory; in `CODES_NOT_NAMED_IN_SPEC`, builder label added); both appear in `GET /harnesses/`. claude-code: `facts_version` 2, `open_questions=()`, the OpenRouter passthrough question moved to `endpoint_open_questions["openrouter"]` (local endpoints use the `anthropic_messages` translator), the missing real-model smoke run to `exploratory_only`, `wants_enabled=True`. goose: `facts_version` 2, the built-in skills caveat moved to `exploratory_only` (the sandbox has no web egress; `tool_policy` stays in `unsupported_knobs`), `wants_enabled=True`. The four claude-code/goose presets are `archived: false` (content unchanged, so their hashes stay; the seeder syncs the flag on `migrate`). `images/agent-claude-code/Dockerfile` ends its root `RUN` with `find /run/home -mindepth 1 -delete`; new tag `llmlab/agent-claude-code:29aaa9df7e99` (`sha256:ae157933…`; first built as `f5bb8dc5c36d` before a comment-only Dockerfile edit, same image id) built on apps-dind through `ensure_image` (HOME empty and node-owned, `claude --version` 2.1.316); the goose image was already clean. Live (django autoreload): both `enabled=True`; for `local-opencode-go/deepseek-v4.1-flash` on `openai_compatible` claude-code gets `harness_exploratory_only` (warn) + `vendor_affinity` (info), plus `output_cap_below_harness_min` (error) at 8192, none at 16384; on OpenRouter it gets `harness_not_enabled`; goose gets only `harness_exploratory_only` (warn). Gateway smoke on apps-dind with the real images (`agent-claude-code:f5bb8dc5c36d` = `sha256:ae157933…`, `agent-goose:1b1deee4bfdc`, `llm-gateway-local:ea63602eeffc`, `--internal` network, `local_proxy` upstream = the scripted chat stand-in): claude-code `anthropic_messages/translate` (2 calls `POST /v1/messages?beta=true` 200, upstream got `/v1/chat/completions`, `hello.txt` written, exit 0); goose `openai_chat/native` (2 calls `/api/v1/chat/completions` 200, `hello.txt`, exit 0). - **E2E-18/19 checks.** `.wt-test.sh e2f4 --create-db`: `backend/execution/tests tests/wp07_harnesses tests/wp01_foundation tests/wp08_agentic tests/wp10_study` 1378 passed / 16 skipped; `tests/wp03_library` 199 passed; ruff check + format clean on the changed files; `.wt-fecheck.sh check` 0 errors / 0 warnings. No migration.
- **E2E-20 (E2E-17 refix, "gateway-runaway") a cut answer that came after the gateway's per-call timeout, false truncations from the cap estimate, and $0 calls without a usage chunk → fixed (executor: `inference/pump.py`, `inference/upstream.py`, `inference/requests.py`; no gateway image change, no migration).** (1) **Wall clock anchored at the row's creation.** `pump.stream_limits` subtracted nothing for the time an `LLMRequest` spent queued (OpenCode Go: `max_concurrency=2` pump slots against `max_active_jobs=3`) or in earlier attempts (a `connect`/`timeout` requeue), so `upstream._read_chat_stream`'s deadline (`started` inside `upstream.call` + wall) could fall after the gateway's own `llm_call_timeout_s` and the run still ended `infra_interrupted`. Now the wall is `min(endpoint.request_timeout_s, llm_call_timeout_s - min(30 s, 10 %) - (now - row.created_at))`, i.e. the deadline is `created_at + policy wall - margin`; the remainder travels as `StreamLimits.call_wall_left_s` (None without a policy wall: then nothing changes). `pump.run_one`: with less than `MIN_CALL_WALL_S` (1 s) left the row fails at once (`timeout`, "the per-call wall clock was spent in the queue and earlier attempts") without an upstream call; an upstream `connect`/`timeout` once the wall is spent (less than 1 s left) is not requeued (`requests.complete(..., requeue=False)`, new keyword, default True so the relay path is unchanged; the error says "not retried"). The gateway then gets the endpoint's error at once instead of waiting for its own recv timeout. (2) **The cap cut is only a runaway backstop.** The cut at `ceil(cap * 1.10)` estimated tokens (3.6 chars/token) fired whenever a provider's real text ran above ~3.96 chars/token, turning the provider's own `stop`/`length` plus exact usage into `length` / `llmlab_cut:max_output_tokens` with an estimate (probe: 90 real tokens, `stop`, cap 100 → cut). `CAP_HEADROOM` is replaced by `CAP_CUT_CHARS_PER_TOKEN = 8.0`: the stream is cut once its output text (content + reasoning + tool-call text) reaches `cap * 8` chars, and **only while the provider has not sent a finish**. Live: deepseek-v4.1-flash ended on its own `length` at 100 tokens after 591-597 chars (5.9-6.0 chars/token, so even 6 would have been too tight). After a finish the reader no longer cuts: the wall clock or a read timeout while waiting for the usage chunk / `[DONE]` returns the finished answer (estimated usage if none came). The wall clock is now the main bound; the reported completion of a cut stays `ceil(chars / 3.6)`. (3) **Estimated usage when the stream has no usage chunk.** `ChatStream.completion` set `usage` only from the stream, so a provider/model ignoring `stream_options.include_usage` booked 0 prompt/completion tokens and list cost 0 and bypassed the token and spend caps. A finished stream without usage now carries the same marked estimate as a cut (`prompt_tokens_estimate` + `ceil(chars / 3.6)`, `llmlab_estimated: true`, no `llmlab_cut`); `ChatStream.estimated_usage()` serves both. Tests (`inference/tests/test_stream_cut.py`, fake models `verbose`, `no-usage`, `finish-then-stall` in `fake_openai_stream.py`): the runaway cut now at `cap * 8` chars; `test_a_provider_that_bounds_its_answer_is_never_cut` (560 chars / 100 tokens, own `length`, exact usage kept); `test_a_stream_without_a_usage_chunk_reports_an_estimated_usage`; `test_a_finished_answer_is_not_cut_while_waiting_for_its_usage`; `test_the_wall_clock_runs_from_the_rows_creation` (100 s queued → 170 s wall; 400 s → 0); `test_a_call_whose_wall_clock_was_spent_queued_fails_fast_without_an_upstream_call` (DB: `failed`/`timeout`, attempt 0, upstream never called); `test_a_timeout_after_the_wall_clock_is_not_requeued` (DB: 10 s policy, 7 s queued, silent upstream → `failed` after ~2 s, attempt 0). `.wt-test.sh e2f1`: `backend/inference/tests` + `backend/generation/tests/wp06_gateway` + `backend/generation/tests/wp12_slot` 663 passed; ruff check + format clean on the 5 changed files. Live probe on dev1 (django container, direct `upstream.call` to OpenCode Go endpoint pk 4, outside any study, < $0.01 list): deepseek-v4.1-flash cap 100 → its own `length`, usage 100 exact, not cut (the old code cut it at 396 chars); qwen3.8-flash cap 400 → `llmlab_cut:max_output_tokens` at 3208 chars (est 892, ~2.5x the cap at qwen's ~3.15 chars/token) in 13.5 s. Not verified live: the queued-row wall and no-requeue path (unit/DB tests only). Known limit: a call failed fast because its wall was spent is still an `endpoint_unavailable` infra error for the run (it fails in seconds instead of at the gateway's timeout; there is no model output to answer with). **Needs the executor drain/restart/resume (Deploy phase)**; the pump runs in the executor.
- **E2E-21 (items 2+3, deploy gap) the E2E-19 preset un-archive never reached dev1 → fixed (ops, no code).** The item-2 resolve of study `03df8fe9` raised `lint_unknown_library_ref` for `claude-code-native` and `goose-native`: the deploy ran `migrate`, but dev1 does not set `GENERATION_SEED_ON_MIGRATE` (default `False`, `docs/development-guide.md`), so the post-migrate seeder never ran and the four claude-code/goose presets stayed `is_archived=True`. `manage.py seed_library --dry-run` listed exactly those four (`~ harness_preset:{claude-code,goose}-{native,controlled}@1`, 290 unchanged); `seed_library --kind harness_preset` applied them (0 created, 4 updated; content hashes unchanged). The ephemeral resolve cache (`plan.CACHE_TTL_S` 600 s, per process) kept serving the old plan until the design changed. Deploy checklist: run `seed_library` (or set the flag) after `migrate` whenever seed files change.

The emitter now sends only the contract's fields. Test `wp10_study/test_automation.py::test_post_run_events_match_the_study_event_contract` (the published event parses with `parse_study_event`); `.wt-test.sh i23a`: `wp10_study` 121 passed; ruff check + format clean. **Needs the executor restart** (not done in this phase).
- **E2E-23 (item 4, full slot pilot) a re-freeze after target validation kept the stale "validation is pending" errors, so a pinned slot study could never launch → fixed (django only; no migration, no executor restart).** Since E2E-14 pins the draw, the re-freeze after the matched targets validated produces the **same** `plan_hash` (target validation and the estimate's priors are diagnostics outside the hash), and `plan.persist_bundle` returned the existing `StudyPlan` row unchanged, whose stored `issues` still held `slot_tests_uninformative` "validation is pending" errors; `launch` then refused with `plan_has_errors` for ever (studies `e3b0eed8` and `0074e180`: 6 matched targets all `ok` after validation, every re-freeze returned plan `bcc2be71…` / `58c40811…` with 2 blocking errors). Also `freeze` resolved through the 10-minute ephemeral cache, which does not see validation finish either. Now `persist_bundle` replaces a stale row (`_stale_draft`: same hash, stored issues or estimate differ from the fresh resolve, never launched, not the active plan, no conditions/units/cells refer to it) and `freeze` resolves with `use_cache=False`. Also fixed in passing: `study/parents.drawn_units` passed `pool_size=result.pool_size` when freezing a missing set of a **pinned** draw, where `result` is unbound (NameError); it now uses `out.pool_size`. Test `wp10_study/test_parent_draw.py::test_a_refreeze_after_target_validation_replaces_the_stale_draft` (freeze with pending matched targets → `plan_has_errors`; targets validated → same hash, new row, no errors, old row gone; an unchanged re-freeze keeps the row; launch succeeds). `.wt-test.sh sp21 --create-db`: `wp10_study` 122 passed; ruff check + format clean. Live through the django autoreload: both studies re-froze without errors and launched (cap $4.00 / $2.50 list).
- **E2E-24 (item 2) a stream call the real upstream refused is charged its full reservation on the local path → found, not fixed (gateway image).** Run `456b8fbf` (study `03df8fe9`, goose-native / flask-react / qwen3.8-flash), attempt 1: calls 28–31 got `opencode-go answered 400: Upstream request failed: [server_error] Upstream response was not valid JSON` within 0.04 s each, yet `calls.jsonl` records `usage: null, usage_estimated: true, list_cost = cost = reserved_usd = 0.01663193` for each (4 × $0.0166 = $0.067 of the attempt's $0.188 list), and the ledger's `adj:…:1` (gateway_final) settles to that total. Cause: for `stream: true` the executor proxy (`inference/proxy_server._stream`) sends `200` + SSE headers at once (it pings while the `LLMRequest` waits), then relays the refusal as an SSE error event; the gateway's `_relay` sets `Outcome.upstream_status = resp.status` (200), so `upstream_billed` is true and `budget_rules.settle` prices the usage-less failed call at its reservation (prompt estimate + the call's full `max_tokens` 16384). The executor proxy's own settle gets the real 400 and charges 0, so the two budgets disagree. Suggested fix: in `_relay`, when the stream's summary error carries an explicit `status >= 400` and no content, tool calls or usage arrived, take that status as `upstream_status` (the upstream refused, not billed); test with the local proxy's 200-then-error stream. Not edited during the run: `image_tag` hashes the gateway source live from the bind mount, so an edit would switch the gateway image mid-study for every running study.
- **E2E-25 (acceptance item 1: conformance kit + oracle validation) go-stdlib and spring-boot were "missing: no runs", and the public-page check of `notes_markdown_render` failed every correct app → fixed (reference trees, compiler; no migration, no LLM spend).**
  - **Why go/spring were missing:** `conformance.run_kit` only evaluates `data/tasks/<kit task>/reference/<stack slug>/`; WP-09 wrote references for the four python/node frameworks only, so every go/spring stack was recorded `missing` (no run). Real `manage.py conformance_kit` on dev1 (2026-11-04 04:12–04:20 UTC, eval-v2, docker runtime): **all 6 `<fw>-none` stacks pass all 3 kit tasks** (go-none runs 7c338d76 / 4cfb0971 / e87f21b7, spring-boot-none d24b829f / b27114a7 / fbcf0cf5; flask-none 2270f041 / 2afbd58a / b75a9bd8; fastapi-none 91f8338f / e7d828d3 / 9c4bb8d5; django-none 696b1e20 / e31d552e / fe664672; express-none dc133af1 / d5878320 / 14d6f285), every probe `not_exploited`; `coverage()` = `{django, express, fastapi, flask, go-stdlib, spring-boot}: true`.

  - **Compiler defect found by the notes oracle → fixed (`evaluation/acceptance.py`).** All three runs of every notes reference failed only `public.render.ok`: the compiled setup creates the note with the boolean sample `published: false`, and the check expects 200 from `GET /api/public/notes/{id}/html`, which the task requires to be 404 for an unpublished note. So every correct app lost that check. `_endpoint_checks` now passes the setup of an `anon` check through `_publicly_visible`, which sends the visibility flags it finds (`_VISIBILITY_FLAGS`: published, public, visible, active, listed, shared and their `is_` forms) as `true`. Across the 43 seeded system tasks and all auth modes this changes exactly `notes_markdown_render public.render.ok` and `ecommerce_cart products.get.ok` (it created an `is_active: false` product, which that task hides; not validated: no ecommerce reference). Owner-only checks (`*.anon_401`) and the three snapshot tasks are byte-identical, so `COMPILER_VERSION` stays 1; no evaluation of either affected task existed on dev1 (the pilot's 60 notes runs were cancelled or pending). The running executor picks it up at its next restart (Deploy phase).

Also fixed two stale `wp04_content` expectations from E2E-19 (`content.ENABLED_HARNESSES` adds goose and claude-code, whose presets are no longer archived). `.wt-test.sh kitfx -- pytest wp09_eval wp04_content`: 688 passed.
