The WP-09 trees keep "No LLM was involved". `docs/EVALUATION.md` has the run summary. Driver scripts and logs: `~/.cache/llmlab-v3-work/e2e/kit/`.
- **E2E-26 (item 4) a one-line ts_query draft "from a selection" was a single token that matched everything → fixed (django only).** `patterns/drafts._covering_node` took the smallest named node whose **rows** covered the selection, so on a one-line selection any token of that line qualified: selecting line 247 `if row is None or not check_password_hash(row["password_hash"], password):` of variant `d485f12e` (study `e3b0eed8`) drafted `(string_start) @match` (every string in every file), and the earlier pilot's line-371 selection drafted `((identifier) @id0 (#eq? @id0 "row")) @match`. The cover is now computed on **bytes**: the node must span the selection from its first to its last non-blank byte (`_selection_bytes`); the same selection now drafts `((if_statement condition: (boolean_operator …) consequence: (block …)) (#eq? @id0 "row")) @match`. Test `wp13_patterns/test_seed_and_invariants.py::test_a_one_line_ts_query_draft_covers_the_whole_line_not_its_first_token`; `.wt-test.sh sp24 --create-db`: `wp13_patterns` 204 passed / 8 skipped; ruff check + format clean. Live through the django autoreload (re-drafted on dev1).
- **E2E config (item 4), not code: OpenCode Go admission raised from 3 to 6 active jobs (pump concurrency 2 → 3).** With the parallel app pilot `03df8fe9` and step-3 study `c7da7756` holding all 3 `max_active_jobs` with 10-20 minute agent runs, the 126 single-shot slot cells of `e3b0eed8` were `endpoint_full` at the head of the queue and finished about one per 5-10 minutes. Agentic runs are bounded by the 3 sandbox slots anyway, so the extra admission goes to sandbox-free single-shot runs; the pump's queue wait on this endpoint over the previous 2 h was p50 0.01 s / p90 8.8 s with no 429 in 6 h, so `max_concurrency` was raised by one. `LLMEndpoint` pk 4: `max_active_jobs` 3 → 6, `max_concurrency` 2 → 3 (the scheduler and the pump's `reconcile` read both live; no restart). After the change the single-shot study ran 2-3 cells at once (~1.5 variants/min). **Reverted** to 3 / 2 at 05:54 UTC once the slot pilot finished.
- **E2E-27 (item 4) the execution queue hid a paused condition and answered 500 on per-study concurrency reasons → fixed (django + frontend types; the executor itself is unchanged).** (1) Study `e3b0eed8`'s qwen3.8-flash condition (68) was paused by the frugal `cost_overrun` rule (pause_condition, list cost 2.9× the estimate: qwen's unbounded reasoning, up to 19 900 tokens per single-shot call, is not in the priors), and its 54 pending runs stood at the head of `GET /api/execution/queue/` with `blocked_reason: null` ("would be admitted") although the lane SQL skips a paused condition, so nothing said why nothing ran. The queue now answers `condition_paused` for runs of a paused condition. (2) The scheduler publishes `study_endpoint_cap` / `study_model_cap` / `study_provider_cap` (per-study `concurrency.per_*`, E2E-18 era) and `budget`, but the `BlockedReason` literal of the queue schema did not list them, so `/queue/` failed response validation (500) as soon as a per-model cap was the reason (seen live once the condition resumed). All five are now in `api_schemas.BlockedReason`, `frontend/src/lib/api/types/execution.ts` and `BLOCKED_REASON_LABELS`. Tests `execution/tests/test_capacity_api.py::TestApi::test_queue_names_a_paused_condition` and `::test_every_scheduler_reason_is_a_queue_reason`; `.wt-test.sh sp27 --create-db`: `backend/execution/tests` 163 passed; ruff check + format clean; `.wt-fecheck.sh check` 0 errors / 0 warnings. **Found, not fixed:** a `pause_condition` rule has no "acknowledge and continue": `resume_condition` re-arms a rule whose window still holds the same evidence, and the policy (threshold) of a launched study cannot change, so a researcher who accepts the overrun must resume the condition again after every re-pause (done for the pilot by a loop calling `POST …/conditions/68/resume/`; it re-paused once in the first minute). A per-condition acknowledgement (rule code + time, counted from then on, like E2E-5's replaced attempts) needs a `StudyCondition` field and a migration.
- **Item 4 (full slot pilot) results and notes (not changed).** Three studies on dev1, total list spend $1.07 (billed $0): `e3b0eed8` (crud, single shot, cap $4: 120 variants, 115 tested / 5 `output_invalid:truncated`, all qwen3.8-flash whose reasoning ran to 19 900 tokens under the 16 384 cap of the derived preset `e2e-slot-pe3@2`; $0.42), `0074e180` (crud, restricted agent opencode-native × deepseek, 4 repeats on 3 parents × 2 targets, cap $2.50: 24 variants, 23 tested; $0.55) and `21ca1839` (notes_markdown_render, single shot, cap $1.50: 80 variants, 79 tested; $0.10). Parents drawn `random k=3 stratified by parent_model` and pinned: crud 573ecd62 (qwen) / 06b619ed (deepseek) / d8e217b6 (qwen); notes 79a7ffc1 (qwen) / 00e2bbbc / b248a338 (deepseek). (a) *Only one notes parent has a markdown component*: the notes acceptance tests are API-only, so 3 of 4 tested notes parents with pass ≥ 0.7 kept the starter frontend (`App.tsx` stub); the template `MarkdownPreview` matched only its own parent and validated `uninformative` (no linked check; the holed tree fails to build), usable in an exploratory study with a warn. The server render handler `GET /api/notes/{id}/html` (`notes.render`, 4 linked checks) was added as a second template and matched into all three parents. (b) The `parent_draw_pending` info issue keeps saying `not_matched_yet` / "freezing the plan … matches the templates" for a template that freeze already tried and could not match (`MarkdownPreview` in 00e2bbbc, b248a338); it should say `not_found` and that a freeze will not help. (c) Two of the three `notes.render` originals leak 99 % / 100 % (the same rendering code sits in the public render handler in context), and their variants are near-copies: exact-duplicate share 0.8-0.9, `low_effective_n` true. (d) `low_effective_n` counts only exact duplicates (spec 7.9): crud login in parent 06b619ed × deepseek has 10 variants in **one** MinHash cluster (top-1 share 1.0) but distinct normalized shas, so it is not flagged; near-duplicate collapse is visible only through `distinct`. (e) A qwen3.8-flash outlier parent ebe9d6fb (opencode) is "tested" with agent pass rate 0.0 and 2 025 files (five `.venvN` directories the agent created are part of the run tree); `min_pass_rate` kept it out of the pool.
- **E2E-28 (item 2) the app pilot was budget-stopped after its first block although the builder said every block fit → fixed (estimator + new issue; django only, no migration).** Study `03df8fe9` (cap $5.00 list, 12 conditions × 3 tasks, 1 repeat, preset `frugal-e2e-item23@2`) stopped at 05:45 with `stop_reason.detail = "cost: 1.65258821 committed + 3.60000000 > cap 5.00000000"`: `budget.claims` reserves a whole replicate block at its worst case when the block's first run is admitted (12 runs × `run.max_cost_usd` 0.30 = $3.60), so block 2 could not start once block 1 had committed $1.65, and the 24 cells of blocks 2–3 ended `excluded:budget_stop` (12 of 36 pilot cells ran). The launch-time estimate had said `fit.blocks_under_cap: 3`, because `_fit` summed only expected block costs. Two defects: (1) **the fit ignored the admission rule**: `_fit` now admits a block only while the expected spend of the blocks before it plus the block's whole reservation (Σ `CellEstimate.cap_usd`, = `ledger.run_need`) fits, over the run cells in admission order (pilot blocks, then main blocks). A cap that admits fewer blocks than planned raises a new `cap_admits_fewer_blocks` warn (registered in `contracts/issues.py`, builder label added, in `CODES_NOT_NAMED_IN_SPEC`) with a fix to the smallest cap that admits every block. (2) **reused pilot cells were counted twice**: under `pilot.reuse_as_main` an exploratory study's pilot cells become its repeat-0 main cells (`expand_pilot`), but `estimate_plan` priced both (`03df8fe9`: 72 cells, expected $6.04 list, the launch confirm asked for a $12.76 p90 for 36 runs). `_run_pairs` drops a main cell whose (condition, unit, repeat) has a pilot cell when the policy reuses pilot cells and the study is exploratory, so totals, `by_condition`, `by_level`, time and worst case count each run once; confirmatory studies and `reuse_as_main: false` are unchanged. `estimate_plan` needs the study kind for that: the resolver passes `study_kind=self.kind` through `StudyDeps.estimate_plan`, and `POST /budget/estimate/` passes the study's or the draft design's kind. Live after the fix (django autoreload): item-3 study `39601f71` resolves to 90 runs (18 pilot + 72 new main) with expected $7.05 list, not 108; at cap $3.60 the fit is 4 blocks, at $4.50 6 blocks. Tests: `wp11_budget/test_estimate.py` (`test_a_cap_that_admits_fewer_blocks_warns`, `test_reused_pilot_cells_are_counted_once`; the fixture fits follow the admission rule: 6→5 blocks at cap 2.0, 3→2 at cap 1.0, plus the new warn next to `estimate_exceeds_cap`). `.wt-test.sh i23b`: `wp11_budget` + `wp01_foundation` 1066 passed; `i23c`: `wp10_study` + `wp12_slot` 266 passed; `i23d`: `wp11_budget` 290 passed after the API change; ruff check + format clean; `.wt-fecheck.sh check` 0 errors / 0 warnings. The executor's admission (`budget.claims`) is unchanged.
- **E2E-29 (item 2) the ledger did not reconcile to the `calls.jsonl` sum → (i) fixed (executor: `budget/ledger.py`), (ii) found, not fixed.** Study `03df8fe9`: ledger usage + adjust $1.421823 list, `calls.jsonl` $1.684107 (−$0.262284). `~/.cache/llmlab-v3-work/e2e/item23/reconcile3.py` splits it exactly (no run with a residual above 1e-7): **(i) −$0.269734**: `ledger._from_calls` (the settlement's final usage) skipped every record with `usage: null` ("a failed call without usage costs nothing billable"), but since E2E-20 the gateway settles such calls at their reservation (`usage_estimated: true`: a stream without a usage chunk, or a failure after the upstream "accepted" it, which includes the E2E-24 refusals), charges `cost`/`list_cost` toward the run cap and writes them to `calls.jsonl` (e.g. `1f08e760`: one estimated record, $0.0755 charged vs $0.0652 booked). `_from_calls` now counts a usage-less record flagged `usage_estimated` at what the gateway charged: `list_cost` toward the list total; `cost` toward billed only when `cost_source` is not `list_price`/`free` (a subscription bills 0); the reserved prompt and `upstream_max_tokens` as tokens; one request. Other usage-less records stay free. Test `wp11_budget/test_ledger.py::test_a_call_settled_at_its_reservation_counts_what_the_gateway_charged`; `.wt-test.sh i23f`: `wp11_budget` 291 passed (`i23e`: + `wp08_agentic` 416 passed before the test was added); ruff clean. **Needs the executor restart**; item-3 study `39601f71` runs on the old code. **(ii) +$0.007450, not fixed**: `966ae77e`'s attempt was settled (`adj`/`rel` at 04:08:26.82) while one `LLMRequest` was still in flight after the agent hit its wall clock; the request finished upstream and `proxy_server.record_ledger_usage` booked `proxy:9a5a1b34…` 16 s later. The spend is real (the upstream answered) but no `calls.jsonl` record exists (the gateway was gone). Suggested fix: when an attempt settles, cancel its queued/leased `LLMRequest`s, or settle only after they finish, and book a late answer as an explicit `adjust` with source `proxy_late`, so the reconciliation is `calls.jsonl + late proxy`. (iii) −4.7e-8: 8-decimal ledger rounding of 9-decimal `list_cost` values.

## Final-study route fixes (C1, C2, route_error_terminated), 2026-11-09

- **C1, OpenCode Go transient error.** `upstream.transient_reason`: only for host `opencode.ai`, a 400, an error-only 200 body, or an in-stream error event (stored as 502 `llmlab_stream_error`) whose error text has `[server_error]` and no `[data_inspection_failed]`. `pump.run_one` re-submits it at most `upstream.OPENCODE_TRANSIENT_RETRIES` (2) times, with 1 s / 3 s backoff, inside the call's per-call wall clock and the row's `deadline_at`, and only while the row is still leased to this claim (never for a cancelled or reconciler-requeued row). Each retry renews the pump lease (`request_timeout_s + 30` from now), so a retry of a call without a per-call wall clock (`probe`, `analysis`) cannot outlast its lease and be run again by another pump. Nothing reaches the gateway before a row completes, so a re-submission cannot duplicate output. The stored answer carries `llmlab_upstream_retries {count, reason, attempts, exhausted}`, which is read into telemetry `local.transient_retries` / `local.transient_retry_reason`. When a retry then ends in a timeout / connect error, the retries are stored on the row too (`response = {llmlab_upstream_retries}`): a failed row keeps them for the telemetry, a requeued row carries them into its next attempt's answer. If the error is still there after the retries, the row is stored as a 502 (`error.code=upstream_transient`, `upstream_status` = the original status). The proxy then answers 503 `upstream_transient` and records it in `execution.infra_errors`. That 503 lets the gateway's own `retry_on: 5xx` re-submit a non-streamed call. A streamed call already has its 200 headers, so it gets the error in-band, as before. Other endpoints and statuses are unchanged.
- **C2, Go receives the effort.** `chat.build_upstream_body` now keeps the canonical `reasoning` object for OpenAI-flavor bodies to `opencode.ai`. `upstream._provider_quirks` (`opencode_reasoning`) then sends `reasoning.effort` as top-level `reasoning_effort` and drops the object. The proxy forwards the object only when the run's frozen `reasoning.mode` is `effort`. Under `provider_default`, a harness's own `reasoning` is still dropped. If a provider rejects the value, the call fails with a 400 whose message carries `chat.REASONING_REJECTED_HINT` (only when the error text names the effort; DeepSeek's thinking-mode `reasoning_content` 400 does not get it). The plan keeps a Go model's effort only when `metadata.supports_reasoning` is true: `manage.py go_reasoning_effort` (`--dry-run`, `--unset`, `--probe`, `--model`, `--force`). By default it flags only the final-study Go rows whose serving provider lists the effort (`high`); the others need `--model` or `--force`. It also writes `metadata.reasoning_modes=["effort"]` (`model_facts` `extra["reasoning_modes"]`), so the policy clamps any other forced mode (`max_tokens`) to `provider_default` (note `reasoning_unsupported`) and `reasoning.exclude` to false (note `reasoning_exclude_unsupported`) on those rows: nothing is recorded that Go is never sent. `--probe` (not with `--dry-run`) makes two calls per row, without and with the effort, and compares the reasoning tokens.
- **Validity note `route_error_terminated` (warn).** `validity.route_error_note` adds this note when an evaluated attempt (usable termination, not `budget_exhausted`) ended on a failed gateway call after at least one successful call. The end is the last `main` call (a failed auxiliary / side call after it does not count); successful calls are counted over all classes. A failed call here is HTTP >= 400, an error answer, or a transport failure; it is not `client_gone`, not a `budget_exhausted` refusal and not a context refusal or overflow (`context_length_exceeded`, "prompt is too long": the proxy's `call.max_input_tokens` cap or the model's context, not the route). The detail is `kind= status= code= call= request_class=main ok_calls= calls= error=`. Outcomes are unchanged. Exposed as study results `leaderboard[].route_error_terminated_in_sample`, as the export `runs.csv` column `route_error_terminated`, and as study-report row field `route_error_terminated` with a validity warning.

## Platform CPU faults (SIGILL) are never the model's; seeded npm re-lock; evaluation supersession, 2026-11-09

- **Trigger.** Run f79e8f11 (flask-react, claude-opus-5-5) was scored `build_fail` twice (evaluations 591/592):
  `vite build` died with "Illegal instruction (core dumped)", exit 132. `@rolldown/binding-linux-x64-gnu@1.2.18`
  (published 2026-11-09) bundles a mimalloc built for x86-64-v3 (`shlx`, BMI2); the host is Ivy Bridge. The
  platform re-lock (`stale_starter_lock`, manifest-only npm resolve) picked 1.2.18 although the model's own lock
  pinned 1.2.16.
- **Outcome `infra_error:platform_cpu`.** `suite.platform_cpu_fault` (SIGILL / "Illegal instruction" / exit 132,
  log or exit code) in the lock, build, start and tester stages -> `stages.<stage>.status=error`,
  `detail.reason=platform_cpu`, evaluation `error`, not measured, not usable (attrition; no pass rate). The build
  check runs before `lock_incomplete`/`build_infra`/`build_fail` and is never retried.
  `DockerEvalRuntime.exit_code` / `LocalProcessRuntime.exit_code` (optional runtime method) report a stopped app.
- **Seeded npm re-lock.** `_platform_lock` passes the tree's v2+ `package-lock.json` next to `package.json`;
  fallback to the manifest-only resolve when the seed fails or does not cover the manifest. Stored locks are not
  touched (re-evaluations never re-resolve). The build key already hashes every context file, so a different lock
  is a different cached image; no Dockerfile or environment changed.
- **Validity note `eval_superseded:<reason>`** (parametric, `PARAMETRIC_NOTE_CODES`) and
  `execution.eval_supersessions`, written by `evaluation.supersede.supersede` / `manage.py
  reevaluate_platform_failures` when an operator re-evaluates a platform-failed run (`--relock` drops a platform
  lock only).
- **Review hardening (same day).** The detector accepts only OS/shell/runtime/builder SIGILL reports, never the
  words in a sample's own text (quoted strings, `Error: Illegal instruction`, `signal.SIGILL`); the build check is
  step-aware (`suite.build_platform_cpu_fault`: the failed step's exit status 132, or a SIGILL report in that
  step's own output), so a SIGILL an earlier step survived never relabels a model's `build_fail`; a lock resolver
  killed by SIGILL is never `lock_unresolvable`; the tester discards checks only when something failed (a clean
  measured result stands, `sigill_in_app_logs`); `platform_cpu` stages record `detail.host_cpu`; the attrition
  table adds `infra_error_by_reason`; a lock dropped by `--relock` stays referenced for blob GC; the command
  records the operator (`--by`) and accepts only `--reason platform_cpu`.
